Evaluation will be done on\nthe following two-stage process in terms of National Treasury Circular No: 53:\nStage 1:\nAssessment of Functionality. Only service providers who achieve a minimum threshold of 60 points on functionality\nwill qualify to proceed to stage two of the Evaluation process. Functionality criteria is as follows:\nCompany Experience (40 Points)\nKey Personnel (30 Points)\nSchedule of Plant and Equipment (15 Points)\nQuality Control Plan (15 Points)\nStage 2:\nEvaluation will be done based on the 80/20 preferential point system, where 80 points will be allocated for price and\n20 points for specific goals. For the appeal to be considered, it\nmust reach the municipality within the fourteen-day appeals period and be addressed to the\nMunicipal Manager. Moreover, the appeal must be copied to the Supply Chain Manager and\nadhere to the following format:\n• Clearly set out the reasons for the appeal,\n• Clearly state in which way the appellant’s rights have been affected by the decision,\n• Clearly state the relief or remedy sought by the appellant, and\n• be accompanied by a copy of the Intention to Award or notification advising the\nTenderer of the decision of the Municipality.\nIf no bona fide appeals have been received within the appeals period, the successful Tenderer\nwill be notified of the Municipality’s acceptance of their bid.\nThe consideration of appeals and, if necessary, the invalidation of any decision made, shall be\ndealt with in terms of Municipality’s appeals process.\nF.3.17 Provide Copies of the Contracts\n“The number of paper copies of the signed contract to be provided by the Employer is ONE.”\nT1.1.3 Additional Conditions of Tender\nCompliance with Occupational Health and Safety Act 1993 and Construction\nRegulations\n(as amended 2014)\nT.1.1.3.1 Tenderers are to note the requirements of the Occupational Health and Safety Act No. 85 of\n1993 and the latest amended Construction Regulations (2014) issued in terms of Section 43 of\nthe Act. The Tenderer shall be deemed to have read and fully understood the requirements of\nthe above Act and Regulations and to have allowed for all costs in compliance therewith.\nIn this regard the Tenderer shall submit with the tender, appended to Health and Safety Plan in\nT2.2 Returnable Documents, a detailed Health and Safety Plan prepared in accordance with\nthe Health and Safety Specification (given in in respect of the Works in order to demonstrate\nthe necessary competencies and resources to perform the construction work all in accordance\nwith the Act and Regulations).\nTP. 19\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART T1: TENDERING PROCEDURES\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nT.1.1.3.2 T.1.2.3.2 Claims Arising after Submission of Tender\nThe additional conditions of tender are:\nNo claim for any extras arising out of any doubt or obscurity as to the true intent and meaning\nof anything\nshown on the Contract Drawings or contained in the Conditions of Contract, Scope of Work\nand Pricing\nData, will be admitted by the Employer/Employer’s Implementing Agent after the submission of\nany tender and the Tenderer shall be deemed to have:\n1. Inspected the Tender Drawings and read and fully understood the Conditions of Contract.\n2. Read and fully understood the whole text of the Scope of Work and Pricing Data and\nthoroughly acquainted himself with the nature of the works proposed and generally of all\nmatters which may influence the Contract.\n1. Visited the site of the proposed works, carefully examined existing conditions, the means of\naccess to the site, the conditions under which the work is to be done, and acquainted himself\nwith any limitations or restrictions that may be imposed by the Municipal or other Authorities\nin regard to access and transport of materials and plant to and from the site and made the\nnecessary provisions for any additional costs involved thereby.\n1. requested the Employer or his duly authorised agent to make clear the actual requirements\nof anything shown on the Tender Drawings or anything contained in the Scope of Work and\nPricing Data, the exact meaning or interpretation of which is not clearly intelligible to the\nTenderer.\nBefore submission of any tender, the Tenderer should check the number of pages, and if any\nare found to be missing or duplicated, or the figures or writing indistinct, or if the Pricing Data\ncontain any obvious\nerrors, the Tenderer must apply to the Employer/Employer’s Implementing Agent at once to\nhave the same rectified, as no liability will be admitted by the Employer/Employer’s\nImplementing Agent in respect of errors in any tender due to the foregoing.\nT.1.1.3.3 Imbalance in Tendered Rates\nTP. 20\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART T1: TENDERING PROCEDURES\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nIn the event of tendered rates or lump sums being declared by the Employer to be\nunacceptable to it\nbecause they are either excessively low or high or not in proper Balance with other rates or\nlump sums,\nthe Tenderer may be required to produce evidence and advance arguments in support of the\ntendered rates or lump sums objected to. If, after submission of such evidence and any further\nevidence requested, the Employer is still not satisfied with the tendered rates or lump sums\nobjected to, it may request the Tenderer to amend these rates and lump sums along the lines\nindicated by it.\nThe Tenderer will then have the option to alter and/or amend the rates and lump sums objected\nto and such other related amounts as are agreed on by the Employer, but this shall be done\nwithout altering the Contract Price.\nShould the Tenderer fail to amend the Tender in a manner acceptable to the Employer, the\nEmployer may reject the Tender.\nT.1.1.3.4 Community Liaison Officer\nDemocratically elected project area members who in turn have representation on the overall\nProject Steering Committee (PSC) represents the residents being served by the project. The\nContractor will be required to liaise through the CLO for any matters to do with the community.\nThe CLO shall be paid an amount of R6 500 per month for the duration of contract.\nT.1.1.3.5 Labour Intensive Construction / Use of Local Labour\nAspects of this project will be carried out in accordance with the provisions of EPWP and as set\nout in clauses F.2.1.3 and F.2.1.4.\nThe contractor must to make use of local labour in order to promote labour intensive\nconstruction, the labour-intensive works to be done under the contract is as stated below:\n• Excavation for shallow foundations\n• Steel fixing for foundations and other reinforced structures\n• Shuttering\n• Bricklaying\n• Building works\n• General cleaning\nRecruitment of labour will be done in accordance with the Greater Kokstad Municipality’s\nEPWP Recruitment Guidelines. The latest gazetted government labour rate for the\nConstruction industry will to be used in the contract for remuneration of local labour.\nTP. 21\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART T2: RETURNABLE DOCUMENTS AND\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3 SCHEDULES\nTABLE OF CONTENTS\nT2: RETURNABLE DOCUMENTS AND SCHEDULES ........................................................................ GENERAL CONDITIONS\n1.1 The following preference point systems are applicable to invitations to\ntender:\n- the 80/20 system for requirements with a Rand value of up to R50 000 000 (all applicable taxes\nincluded); and\n- the 90/10 system for requirements with a Rand value above R50 000 000 (all applicable taxes\nincluded).\n1.2 To be completed by the organ of state\n(delete whichever is not applicable for this tender).\na) The applicable preference point system for this tender is the 80/20 preference point system.\nb) Either the 80/20 preference point system will be applicable in this tender. The lowest/ highest\nacceptable tender will be used to determine the accurate system once tenders are received.\n1.3 Points for this tender (even in the case of a tender for income-generating\ncontracts) shall be awarded for:\n(a) Price; and (b)\nSpecific Goals.\n1.4 To be completed by the organ of state:\nThe maximum points for this tender are allocated as follows:\nPOINTS\nPRICE 80\nSPECIFIC GOALS 20\nTotal points for Price and SPECIFIC GOALS 100\n1.5 Failure on the part of a tenderer to submit proof or documentation required\nin terms of this tender to claim points for specific goals with the tender, will\nbe interpreted to mean that preference points for specific goals are not\nclaimed.\n1.6 The organ of state reserves the right to require of a tenderer, either before a\ntender is adjudicated or at any time subsequently, to substantiate any claim\nin regard to preferences, in any manner required by the organ of state.\n1. DEFINITIONS\n(a) “tender” means a written offer in the form determined by an organ of state in response to an\ninvitation to provide goods or services through price quotations, competitive tendering process or any\nother method envisaged in legislation;\n(b) “price” means an amount of money tendered for goods or services, and includes all applicable taxes\nless all unconditional discounts;\n---\n_ RD. 6\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART T2: RETURNABLE DOCUMENTS AND THE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nSCHEDULES\n(c) “rand value” means the total estimated value of a contract in Rand, calculated at the time of bid\ninvitation, and includes all applicable taxes;\n(d) “tender for income-generating contracts” means a written offer in the form determined by an\norgan of state in response to an invitation for the origination of income-generating contracts through\nany method envisaged in legislation that will result in a legal agreement between the organ of state\nand a third party that produces revenue for the organ of state, and includes, but is not limited to,\nleasing and disposal of assets and concession contracts, excluding direct sales and disposal of\nassets through public auctions; and\n(e) “the Act” means the Preferential Procurement Policy Framework Act, 2000 (Act No. 5 of 2000).\n1. Schedule of Labour Content\nThe Tenderer shall be required to participate in job creation (employment of local labour) by executing\nvarious portions of the Works using local labour (unskilled or semi-skilled), recruited from the local\ncommunity, who are South African Citizens or foreigners in possession of a work visa issued by the\nDepartment of Home Affairs (only one such foreigner may be employed on any project). Proof of citizenship\nor work visa may be audited during the contract period.\nThe creation of one job shall mean the employment, for any period of time, of one such unskilled or\nsemiskilled labourer from the local community.\nThe Tenderer shall note the requirements for Job Creation Reporting for EPWP as set out in Part E:\nExpanded Public Works Programme (EPWP) of the project specifications.\nThe number of jobs to be created using such local labour is inclusive of the local labour employed to execute\nvarious portions of the Works by both the main Contractor and any subcontractors, including the small\ndevelopment subcontractors in terms of Part F: Small Contractor Development of the project specifications.\nThe number of jobs to be created using such local labour shall include for a minimum percentage allocation\nto the following individual targeted groups:\n• 60% Women;\n• 55% Youth; and\n• 2% Disabled.\nThe minimum required content of such local labour for this project shall be calculated as follows:\nMinimum required content of such local labour (%)\n= (100 x amount spent on wages for such local labour (excluding VAT))\n(Subtotal 1* (excluding contingencies, contract price adjustment and VAT))\n*Subtotal 1 is obtained from the Tender Summary under in the Bill of Quantities\nThe minimum required content of such local labour for this project shall be 7.71%.\nFor purposes of completing the table on the next page containing the Tenderer’s declaration with respect to\nparticipation in job creation using local labour, the value of Subtotal 1* (excluding contingencies, contract\nprice adjustment and VAT) which is obtained from the Tender Summary under C2.2 Bill of Quantities, shall\nbe used.\nTENDERER’S DECLARATION WITH RESPECT TO PARTICIPATION IN JOB CREATION USING LOCAL\nLABOUR:\nThe Tenderer shall complete the table below reflecting the anticipated local labour force to be employed on\nthis contract, including The Contractor shall have no entitlement under Clause 5.12 to an\nextension of time for Practical Completion.\nWhere the permit to do construction work is not received within the 14-day period following Commencement\nof the Contract for reasons not attributable to the Contractor, the Employer’s Agent shall delay the instruction\nto commence the Works and the Contractor shall be entitled to make a claim in accordance with Clause\n10.1.”\nC. 11\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART C1: AGREEMENTS AND THE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - REHABILITATION WARD CONTRACT DATA\nC1.6: FUNCTIONALITY CRITERIA\nThis section must be read in conjunction with Clause F3.11.9 in T1.2 TENDER DATA.\nC1.6.1 Target Values - Tender Evaluation Criteria\nThe Procedure for the evaluation of responsive tenders is Method 2: Price and Preference. Quality\n(functionality) will be used as a prequalifying criterion, with tenderers required to meet a minimum quality\nscore of 60% in order to qualify for further evaluation.\nEach evaluation criteria will be assessed in terms of five indicators and scores allocated according to the\nfollowing:\nC1.6.1.1: Schedule of work carried out by Tenderer\nCompany Experience - Form B4.1\nC1.6.1.2: Schedule of plant and equipment\nSchedule of plant and equipment - Form B4.2\nC1.6.1.3: Proposed Key Personnel\nProposed Key Personnel - Form B4.3\nCertified qualification certificates and ID copies of ALL key personnel must be attached and are required to\nclaim any points.\nThe submitted CVs must be signed by the proposed personnel, as well as the Tenderer’s authorized signatory,\nto claim points. The CV template is attached as FORM B4.3. This format MUST be used to claim points. PD. 5\nPD. 1\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C2: PRICING DATA THE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nC2.1 PRICING INSTRUCTIONS\n1 The Conditions of Contract, the Contract Data, the Specifications (including the Project Specifications)\nand the Drawings shall be read in conjunction with the Bill of Quantities.\n2 The Bill comprises items covering the Contractor's profit and costs of general liabilities and of the\nconstruction of Temporary and Permanent Works.\nAlthough the Tenderer is at liberty to insert a rate of his own choosing for each item in the Bill, he should\nnote the fact that the Contractor is entitled, under various circumstances, to payment for additional work\ncarried out and that the Engineer is obliged to base his assessment of the rates to be paid for such\nadditional work on the rates the Contractor inserted in the Bill. Clause 8 of each Standardized\nSpecification, and the measurement and payment clause of each Particular Specification, read together\nwith the relevant clauses of the Project Specifications, all set out which ancillary or associated activities\nare included in the rates for the specified operations\n3 Descriptions in the Bill of Quantities are abbreviated and may differ from those in the Standardized and\nProject Specifications. No consideration will be given to any claim by the Contractor submitted on such a\nbasis. The Bill has been drawn up generally in accordance with the latest issue of Civil Engineering\nQuantities. Should any requirement of the measurement and payment clause of the appropriate\nStandardized or Project Specification(s) be contrary to the terms of the Bill or, when relevant, to the Civil\nEngineering Quantities, the requirement of the appropriate Standardized, Project, or Particular\nSpecification as the case may be, shall prevail\n4 Unless stated to the contrary, items are measured net in accordance with the Drawings without any\nallowance having been made for waste.\n5 The amounts and rates to be inserted in the Bill of Quantities shall be the full inclusive amounts to the\nEmployer for the work described under the several items. Such amounts shall cover all the costs and\nexpenses that may be required in and for the construction of the work described, and shall cover the\ncosts of all general risks, profits, taxes (but excluding value-added tax), liabilities and obligations set forth\nor implied in the documents on which the Tender is based.\n6 An amount or rate shall be entered against each item in the Bill of Quantities, whether or not quantities\nare stated. An item against which no amount or rate is entered will be considered to be covered by the\nother amounts or rates in the Bill.\nThe Tenderer shall also fill in a rate against the items where the words "rate only" appear in the amount\ncolumn. Although no work is foreseen under these items and no quantities are consequently given in the\nquantity column, the tendered rates shall apply should work under these items actually be required.\nShould the Tenderer group a number of items together and tender one sum for such group of items, the\nsingle tendered sum shall apply to that group of items and not to each individual item, or should he\nindicate against any item that full compensation for such item has been included in another item, the rate\nfor the item included in another item shall be deemed to be nil.\nThe tendered rates, prices and sums shall, subject only to the provisions of the Conditions of Contract,\nremain valid irrespective of any change in the quantities during the execution of the Contract.\n7 The quantities of work as measured and accepted and certified for payment in accordance with the\nConditions of Contract, and not the quantities stated in the Bill of Quantities, will be used to determine\npayments to the Contractor. The validity of the Contract shall in no way be affected by differences\nbetween the quantities in the Bill of Quantities and the quantities certified for payment.\nPD. 2\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C2: PRICING DATA THE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nOrdering of materials is not to be based on the Bill of Quantities, but only on information issued\nfor construction purposes.\n8 For the purposes of this Bill of Quantities, the following words shall have the meanings hereby assigned\nto them:\nUnit : The unit of measurement for each item of work as defined in\nthe Standardized, Project or Particular Specifications\nQuantity : The number of units of work for each item\nRate : The payment per unit of work at which the Tenderer tenders to do the work\nAmount : The quantity of an item multiplied by the tendered rate of the (same) item\nSum : An amount tendered for an item, the extent of which is\ndescribed in the Bill of Quantities, the Specifications or elsewhere, but of which the\nquantity of work is not measured in units\n9 The units of measurement indicated in the Bill of Quantities are metric units. The following abbreviations\nmay appear in the Bill of Quantities:\nmm = millimetre m = metre\nkm = kilometre\nkm-pass = kilometre-pass m² = square\nmetre\nm²-pass = square metre-pass\nha = hectare\nm³ = cubic metre\nm³-km = cubic metre-kilometre\nkW = kilowatt\nkN = kilonewton kg\n= kilogram t =\nton (1 000 kg)\n% = per cent\nMN = meganewton\nMN-m = meganewton-metre\nPC Sum = Prime Cost Sum\nProv Sum = Provisional Sum\n10 Payment for the Labour-Intensive Component of the Works:\nThose parts of the works to be constructed using labour-intensive methods are marked in the bill of\nquantities with the letters LI either in a separate column or as a prefix or suffix against every item so\ndesignated. The works, or parts of the works so designated are to be constructed using labour-intensive\nmethods only. The use of plant to provide such works, other than plant specifically provided for in the\nscope of work, is a deviation from the contract. The items marked with the letters LI are not necessarily an\nexhaustive list of all the activities which must be done by hand and this clause does not over-ride any of\nthe requirements in the generic labour-intensive specification in the Scope of Works.\nWhere minimum labour intensity is specified in the design, the contractor is expected to use their initiative\nto identify additional activities that can be done labour-intensively in order to comply with the set minimum\nlabour intensity targets.\nPD. 3\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C2: PRICING DATA THE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nPayment for items which are designated to be constructed labour-intensively (either in this schedule or in\nthe Scope of Works) will not be made unless they are constructed using labour-intensive methods. Any\nunauthorised use of plant to carry out work which was to be done labour-intensively will not be condoned\nand any works so constructed will not be certified for payment. Any non-payment for such works shall not\nrelieve the Contractor in any way from his obligations either in contract or in delict\n1. Linkage of Payment for Labour-Intensive Component of Works to Submission of Project Data\nThe Contractor's payment invoices shall be accompanied by labour information for the corresponding\nperiod in a format specified by the employer. If the contractor chooses to delay submitting payment\ninvoices, labour returns shall still be submitted as per frequency and timeframes stipulated by the\nEmployer. The contractor's invoices shall not be paid until all pending labour information has been\nsubmitted. The client may institute a penalty relating to outstanding labour information.\nThe following information shall be maintained on site and submitted in electronic/hard copy formats:\n• Certified ID copies of all locally employed labour\n• Signed Contracts between the employer and the EPWP Participants\n• Attendance Registers for the EPWP Participants\n• Proof of Payment of EPWP Employees\n• Monthly Reporting Template as per EPWP requirements\nPD. 4\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C2: PRICING DATA THE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nC2.2 SCHEDULE OF QUANTITIES\nPD. 5\n\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nCONTRACT No. GKM 05-26/27\nREFERS\nITEM NO. SW.\n50\nSW. 6\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\n• C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nC3.1: STANDARD SPECIFICATIONS\nThe Standard Specifications on which this contract is based are the Standard Specifications for\nRoad and Bridge Works for State Road Authorities, COTO (2020).\nC3.2: PROJECT SPECIFICATIONS\nThe Project Specifications, consisting of two parts, form an integral part of the Contract and\nsupplement the Standard Specifications.\nPart A contains a general description of the Works, the Site and the requirements to be met.\nPart B contains variations, amendments and additions to the Standard Specifications and, if\napplicable, the Particular Specifications.\nIn the event of any discrepancy between a part or parts of the Standard or Particular\nSpecifications and the Project Specifications, the Project Specifications shall take precedence. In\nthe event of a discrepancy between the Specifications (including the Project Specifications) and\nthe drawings and / or the Schedule of Quantities, the discrepancy shall be resolved by the\nEmployer’s Agent before the execution of the work under the relevant item.\nThe Standard Specifications, which form part of this contract, have been written to cover all\nphases of work normally required for road contracts, and they may therefore cover items not\napplicable to this particular contract.\nSW. 7\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nPART A: GENERAL\n[NOTE: This description is a broad outline of the Contract Works and does not limit the work to\nbe executed by the Contractor in terms of the contract. The quantities of some of the major items\nindicated in this section are indicative, not absolute, and are provided to define in general terms\nthe overall scope of the project. [For Compiler information purposes only]\nC3.2.1 Employer’s Objective and Overview of the works\nGreater Kokstad Municipality: The objectives of the project are to improve level of service by\nenhancing the mobility of the road through elimination of potholes and improving riding quality\nthat serves the community of Greater Kokstad Municipality.\nThe scope of works will include improving the stormwater drainage by replacing existing small\npipe culverts with bigger pipe culverts. the scope also entails removing existing pavement layer\nworks up to ± 260mm and stockpiling the material to be used as a selected subgrade layer. there\nwill be importation of surface, base and subbase layer works from commercial sources. The\nworks will also include installation of sidewalks on both side of the road. there will also be\ninstallation of concrete kerbs.\nThe employer's objectives are also to deliver public infrastructure and services using\nlabourintensive methods in accordance with EPWP Guidelines.\nLabour-intensive works comprise the activities such as those described in SANS 1921-5,\nEarthworks activities which are to be performed by hand, and its associated specification data.\nSuch works shall be constructed using local workers who are temporarily employed in terms of\nthis Scope of Work.\nThe Contractor shall be required to attend meetings of the local Project Liaison Committee (PLC)\nfrom time to time. Requirements of the Expanded Public Works Programme (EPWP) are\ncontained in Part F of section C3.3 Particular Specifications. These requirements include\nprovisions for the National Youth Service programme. Requirements in terms of Government’s\ninitiatives for broad based black economic empowerment with respect to small contractor\ndevelopment are contained in Part G of section C3.3 Particular Specifications.\nC3.2.2 Overview of the works\nThe project comprises the rehabilitation of approximately 800 m of Avenue Road in Ward 3 to\nimprove road safety, riding quality and stormwater management. The works include the removal\nand reconstruction of the pavement layerworks, asphalt surfacing, construction of new\nstormwater drainage infrastructure comprising a minimum 900 mm diameter pipe network with\nassociated kerb inlets, catch pits and connections to the existing stormwater system where\nrequired, construction of concrete kerbs, provision of pedestrian sidewalks, upgrading of parking\narea next to the sportfield entrance, road markings, road signs and all associated ancillary works.\nSW. 8\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nProposed Pavement structure for Avenue Street is shown in table C3-1\nFigure C3-1\nTable C3-1: Typical Avenue Road Pavement Structure\nGranular subbase\n30mm AC\n150mm G2 Base\n150mm G5 subbase\n150mm G7 selected subgrade\nIn-situ roadbed\nTable C3-2: Typical Avenue Road Sidewalk Pavement Structure\nGranular subbase\n60mm Paving Block\n20mm Bedding sand\n150mm G5 subbase\n150mm G7 selected subgrade\nIn-situ roadbed\nThe project comprises the rehabilitation of approximately 0.8 km of Avenue Road in Ward 3\nwithin the Greater Kokstad Municipality. The objective of the project is to improve the structural\ncondition of the existing roadway, enhance road safety and upgrade the stormwater drainage\nsystem.\nThe Works include, but are not limited to, the following:\n• Site establishment and traffic accommodation;\n• Rehabilitation of approximately 0.8 km of the existing Avenue Road;\n• Excavation to roadbed level where required;\n• Construction of pavement layerworks comprising G5 subbase and G2 base in accordance\nwith the approved pavement design and project specifications;\n• Placement of a 30 mm asphalt surfacing;\nSW. 9\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\n• Construction of new stormwater drainage infrastructure comprising 600 TO 1500mm\ndiameter concrete pipes, together with associated headwalls, wingwalls, kerb inlets, catch\npits and connections to the existing stormwater system where required;\n• Construction of a 1.5 m wide pedestrian sidewalk on the left-hand side of Avenue\nRoad, extending from the intersection of R56 and Avenue Road to Murray Street;\n• Construction of parking Area ( 732sqm) next to entrance of sportfield\n• Construction of concrete kerbs and side channels;\n• Installation of road traffic signs and road markings; and\n• Testing of materials and workmanship in accordance with the project specifications.\nC3.2.3 Location of site and access\nThe site is located in Kokstad town, within the Greater Kokstad local Municipality (GKM) under\nHarry Gwala district municipality. The street is located in ward 3 of the municipality, as depicted in\nthe Locality Plan.\nC3.2.4 Ground and Subsoil Investigations\nGeotechnical investigations are ongoing on the site and the details of the subsoil conditions will\nbe provided to the successful tenderer. Requests may be made via email by tenderers should\nthey require a copy of the report.\nC3.2.5 Ancillary works\nThe existing asphalt surfaced road is to be removed, the layer-works boxed out and new\nlayerworks and surfacing constructed as depicted in the issued tender drawings.\nC3.2.6 Climatic conditions\nThe climate data obtained from South African Weather Services has shown that Kokstad has\nhigh summer rainfall and cold winters.\na) Temperature\nThe monthly temperature trends for the Kokstad weather station are shown in Figure 0-1. The\nproject area is characterized by hot summers and cold winters. Monthly mean maximum\ntemperatures range between 18.8°C and 26.3°C.\nFigure 0-1, shows that during winter (June to August), the average minimum temperatures drop to\nnearly 1.3°C and there are some extreme cases where the minimum temperature drops to below\nfreezing point, down to almost – 5.2°C. During the spring and summer seasons (September to\nFebruary) the average maximum temperature increases to ±33°C. The extreme maximum\ntemperatures reach 33.7°C in summer and 27.3 °C in winter.\nSW. 10\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nFigure 0-1: Average daily temperatures, per month, for the area (Kokstad weather station) b)\nRainfall\nRainfall data for the Kokstad weather station is summarized in Figure 2-4. The mean annual\nprecipitation (MAP) is 869.2 mm with approximately 89% of this total occurring between October and\nApril, with October and April having almost the same average rainfall. During the rainy season\n(October to April), the average monthly rainfall ranges from 66.6 mm to 153.4mm and during the\nwinter months (June to August) the average rainfall ranges between 11.4mm to 29mm.\nSW. 11\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nFigure 0-2: Summary of rainfall data for the Kokstad weather station\nC3.2.7 Labour\nA Project Liaison Committee shall be established and is a vital means of communication between\nall parties involved with the project. The composition of the PLC shall comprise representatives of\nthe Employer, the Engineer and formal structures within the community.\nThe Contractor shall make use of these communication channels and shall appoint from amongst\nhis site personnel a responsible person to participate in the affairs of the PLC, and this\nrepresentative will be also required to attend the monthly PLC meetings.\nIt is mandatory that the Contractor shall interact with the community via proactive project liaison\nand project participation by its leaders and constituted organisations and forums, as well as\nthrough the employment of its people, and these activities shall constitute essential facets of the\nproject.\nLocal labour is to be used and the employment of such labour is to be done in conjunction with\nthe PLC. The PLC has formed a Labour Committee who shall assist the Contractor with the\nrecruitment of local labourers to ensure an equal distribution of people employed between the\nvarious Traditional Leaders in the area.\nLabour-intensive construction shall mean the economically efficient employment of as great a\nportion of labour as is technically feasible to produce a standard of construction as demanded by\nthe specifications with completion by the Due Completion Date, thus bringing about the effective\nsubstitution of labour for plant and equipment.\nAppropriate portions of the Works included in the Contract shall be executed using\nlabourintensive construction methods.\nSW. 12\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nE5CEpt where the use of plant is essential in order, in the opinion of the Employer’s Agent, to\nmeet the specified requirements by the Due Completion Date, or where the use of plant is\nessential as a result of occupational health and safety considerations, the Contractor shall use\nonly hand tools and equipment in the construction of those portions of the Works that are\nrequired in terms of these Project Specifications to be constructed using labour-intensive\nconstruction methods.\nThese portions of the Works shall be constructed utilizing only locally employed labour and/or the\nlabour of local subcontractors, supplemented by the Contractor’s key personnel to the extent\nnecessary and unavoidable, unless otherwise instructed by the Employer’s Agent and in\naccordance with the further provisions of the relevant sections of Portion B of the Project\nSpecifications.\nSubject to considerations of occupational health and safety, the portions of the Works to be\nexecuted using labour-intensive construction methods are:\n• Site establishment;\n• Proving and relocation of existing services including water, sewer and electricity;\n• Constructions of approximately 0.8km long x (11m tampering to 9m) wide collector road using\nimported material base(G2) and subbase (G5).\n• 30mm Asphalt;\n• Excavation for structures up to 1,5 m deep;\n• Bedding, selected fill, backfilling and compaction of all pipe trenches irrespective of depth,\nbut assisted by mechanical compaction equipment in order to achieve the specified densities;\n• Hauling and spoiling of all excavated materials.\n• Erosion protection;\n• Traffic accommodation through the supply an erect temporary and permanent road signs;\n• Dismantling and re-erection of property fences;\n• Mixing and placing of concrete; for Kerb and Channel (Kerbs to be sourced an to conform\nwith the specifications), concrete for channel can be mixed on site without compromising the\nrequired started;\n• Supply and lay concrete pipe culverts;\n• Installation of subsoil drainage\n• Construction of headwalls, wingwalls, inlet and outlets;\n• Construction of all brickwork required for structures;\n• Road marking;\n• Cleaning and tidying up of the Site and\n• Landscaping.\nIn respect of those portions of the Works which are not listed above, the construction methods\nadopted and the plant utilized shall be at the discretion of the Contractor, provided always that\nthe construction methods adopted and the plant utilized by the Contractor are appropriate in\nrespect of the nature of the Works to be executed and the standards to be achieved in terms of\nthe Contract.\nTask Based Activities: Labour Intensive activities are to be planned as task-based works where\nrequired. Task based refers to a specific amount of work to be performed which is clearly defined\nby a quantity and quality. Typically, a particular task can be completed within a working day.\nC.3.2.8 Spoiling of surplus material\nSurplus material shall be spoiled in designated areas approved by the Employer’s Agent. The\nspoil material shall be disposed of in accordance with subclauses in COTO standard\nspecifications.\nC.3.2.9 Material sources, spoil and stockpile areas\nSW. 13\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nWhere possible, the contractor shall source material from within 20km of the site utilizing local\nsuppliers. The material which may be sourced locally:\n Cement;\n River sand (Coarse Grade Nom. 4.75mm); and\n Asphalt surfacing\nG2, G5, G6 and G7 (portion of material) material shall be obtained from commercial sources.\nSome of G7 will be available from the existing road prism. Spoil areas and areas for the\ntemporary stockpiling of construction materials shall be determined and agreed on site in\nconjunction with the Engineer. The Contractor shall be permitted to use only these agreed spoil\nand stockpile areas, which shall be landscaped and vegetated on completion of the work.\nSpoil areas and areas for the temporary stockpiling of construction materials shall be determined\nand agreed on site in conjunction with the Engineer, the PLC (where applicable) and the local\ncommunities (where applicable). The Contractor shall be permitted to use only these agreed\nspoil and stockpile areas, which shall be landscaped and vegetated on completion of the work.\nInformation regarding a local commercial quarry is shown on\nTable C3-3: Commercial quarries information\nSUPPLIER AVAILABLE PARENT ROCK CONTACT No. Distance from\nMATERIAL the D 3545 Site\nDorning Group G1, G2, G3, G5, G6, N/A 039 7271976 ± 7km\nG7&G9\nC.3.2.10 Accommodation of traffic\nThe accommodation of traffic forms an integral part of the contract and traffic management plan\nto be approved by the Contract Engineer or his representatives onsite.\nC.3.2.11 Existing services\nThe Contractor shall make himself acquainted with the position of all existing services before any\nexcavation or other work likely to affect the existing services has commenced.\nIt is also expected that unknown domestic services crossings requiring relocation or protection\nmay be encountered along the route as the work proceeds. The Contractor shall make every\neffort to establish the location of these services in any area prior to excavations commencing in\nthat area. Such efforts shall include diligent enquiry and discussions with adjacent landowners,\nvisual surface inspection and exploratory trenching investigation as necessary.\nThe Contractor will be held responsible for any damage to known existing services caused by or\narising out of his operations and any damage shall be made good at his own expense. Damage\nto unknown services shall be repaired as soon as possible and liability shall be determined on\nsite when such damage should occur.\nC.3.2.12 Employer’s Agent’s site offices and laboratory\nSW. 14\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nThe Contractor shall be required to provide furnished site office facilities, laboratory facilities,\nablution facilities, and carports for the Engineer at a site located in close proximity to the\nContractor’s office establishment and to the Works. A possible area for such site establishment\nshall be indicated by the Engineer before the commencement of construction.\nC.3.2.13 Temporary Works\nVertical, horizontal and inclined falsework and formwork will be used during the construction of\nthe culvert structures. Provisions of access scaffolding and temporary propping will also be\nrequired. Temporary shoring will be utilised on unstable excavations.\nThe Temporary Works required under this Contract shall include the traffic accommodation\nmeasures implemented, the provision of drainage control, falsework and formwork during the\nconstruction of the minor drainage structures, and the provision of any scaffolding or temporary\npropping used during the erection of the road signs.\nAll Temporary Works shall be removed from the Site on completion of the Contract.\nC.3.2.14 Maintenance of the Works during the construction period\nThe Contractor shall take note of the various requirements of the General Conditions of Contract\n2015 and the standard specifications with respect to the care and protection of the Works.\nThe handing-over of the road reserve for this contract is described in COTO. The Contractor shall\nbe responsible for maintaining this portion of the road from the date of hand-over until the issue\nof the Certificate of Practical Completion.\nC.3.2.15 Testing of materials\nA provisional sum has been allowed in chapter 20 of the Schedule of Quantities for all\nacceptance control testing laboratory work to be carried out by the Engineer using the laboratory\nfacilities included in section 1.4 and/or commercial laboratory facilities.\nThe Contractor shall carry out at his own cost the required process control testing as specified in\nterms of the COTO standard specifications.\nC.3.2.16 Power supply and other services\nThe Contractor shall make his own arrangements concerning the supply of electrical power and\nall other services. No direct payment shall be made for the provision of electrical and other\nservices. The cost thereof shall be deemed to be included in the rates and amounts tendered for\nthe various items of work for which these services are required.\nC.3.2.17 Construction in confined areas\nIt may be necessary for the Contractor to work within confined areas. Except where provided for\nin the specifications, no additional payment shall be made for work done in restricted areas. In\ncertain places the width of the fill material and pavement layers may decrease to zero and the\nworking space may be confined. The method of construction in these confined areas largely\ndepends on the Contractor’s constructional plant.\nHowever, the Contractor shall note that, unless otherwise provided for in terms of the scheduled\npayment items in the COTO Standard Specifications or these project specifications,\nmeasurement and payment shall be in accordance with the specified cross sections and\ndimensions only, irrespective of the method used for achieving these cross sections and\nSW. 15\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\ndimensions, and that the tendered rates and amounts shall include full compensation for all\nspecial equipment and construction methods and for all difficulties encountered when working in\nconfined areas and narrow widths, and at or around obstructions, and that no extra payment shall\nbe made nor shall any claim for additional payment be considered in such cases.\nC.3.2.18 Contractor’s campsite\nPossible locations for a campsite shall be pointed out at the clarification meeting.\nThe Contractor shall make his own arrangements for the provision of his campsite and housing\nfor construction personnel but the chosen site shall be subject to the approval of the Engineer,\nthe local authorities and, where applicable, the Project Liaison Committee (PLC) associated with\nthe project.\nThe standard of the Contractor's camp, offices, accommodation, ablution, and other facilities\nmust comply with the requirements of all local authority, environmental and industrial regulations\nconcerned. In establishing and maintaining his campsite, due cognisance is to be taken of the\nrequirements of clause B1233 of these Project Specifications.\nThe Contractor is to fully familiarise himself with all local by-laws and Government regulations for\nthe employment, transport and accommodation of labour on site.\nThe Contractor shall particularly note that there is a high risk of theft, vandalism and damage to\nproperty in this area and strict security will be required for all plant, establishment, temporary\nworks and partially completed works. The Contractor shall be responsible for providing security\nfor all plant, establishment, temporary works and partially completed works.\nNo separate payment shall be made for the provision of such security since full compensation for\nthese costs shall be deemed to be included in the amount tendered for items in subsection C1.3\n(CONTRACTOR’S SITE ESTABLISHMENT AND GENERAL OBLIGATIONS).\nC.3.2.19 Additional requirements for construction activities\nThe travelling public shall have the right of way on public roads, and the Contractor shall make\nuse of approved methods to control the movement of his equipment and vehicles so as not to\nconstitute a hazard on the road.\nThe Contractor’s tendered rates shall include full compensation for all costs which may arise from\nthe construction and maintenance of deviations and construction under traffic. No claim for\nadditional costs which may arise from these methods of traffic accommodation and no additional\npayment owing to inconvenience as a result of the Contractor’s method of working shall be\nconsidered.\nC3.2.20 Design\n• The Contractor is responsible for the Temporary Works, Site Camp and Contractor’s office\naccommodation design.\n• Site layouts for the Engineer Representative’s temporary office accommodation and a\nMonthly Meeting venue.\nThe costs of the designs will be deemed to have been included in the scheduled items in the\nSchedule of Quantities. No other additional payments will be certified to cover these activities.\nThe bid drawings are applicable to the contractor are detailed in this tender document. These\ndrawings have been used for setting up the Bills of Quantities.\nSW. 16\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nC3.2.21 Quality Assurance (QA) (Read with SANS 1921 – 1: 2004 clause 4.4)\nThe Contractor will be solely responsible for the production of work that complies with the\nSpecifications to the satisfaction of the Engineer. To this end it will be the full responsibility of the\nContractor to institute an appropriate Quality Assurance (QA) system on site. The Engineer will\naudit the Contractor's quality assurance (QA) system on a regular basis to verify that adequate\nindependent checks and tests are being carried out and to ensure that the Contractor's own\ncontrol is sufficient to identify any possible quality problems which could cause a delay or failure.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that efficient supervisory staff, the required transport, instruments,\nequipment and tools are available to control the quality of his own workmanship in accordance\nwith his QA-system. His attention is drawn to the fact that it is not the duty of the Engineer or the\nEngineer’s representative to act as foreman or surveyor.\nC3.2.22 Programme in terms of Clause 5.6 of the General Conditions of Contract\nIt is essential that the construction programme, which shall conform in all respects to Clause 5.6\nof the General Conditions of Contract, be furnished within the time stated in the Contract Data.\nThe preliminary programme to be submitted with the tender shall be used as basis for this\nprogramme.\nC.3.2.23 Construction programme\nIn addition to any other restrictions accommodated by the Contractor in compiling the\nconstruction programme, the following constraints shall be considered in the preparation thereof:\n(1) The whole of the Works (and the portions of the Works if completion in portions is required)\nshall be completed within the time period(s) stated (refer to the Contract Data in section\nC1.2.2).\n(2) Working days lost due to abnormal rainfall shall be treated as set out in clause A1.2.3.4\n(3) Allowance shall be made for non-working days and special non-working days (refer to the\nContract Data in section C1.2.2).\n(4) Construction activities must comply with all the specified environmental requirements including\nPart C: Environmental Management Specification contained in section C3.3 Particular\nSpecifications.\n(5) Construction activities must comply with all the specified health and safety obligations including\nthe requirements of the OHSA 1993 Health and Safety Specifications.\n(6) Strict control of access to and from local public roads shall be required when construction\nvehicles, plant or equipment leave or enter the site.\n(7) Throughout the contract period traffic must be accommodated through the site and all other\ncontractors engaged on the AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION-WARD 3 must be\naccommodated.\n(8) The Contractor’s programme of work shall take due cognisance of risks by limiting the duration\nof the exposure of the various construction elements to natural phenomena.\n(9) Programme must clearly indicate work carried out by the nominated sub-contractors and its\nimpact on the contractors works programme\nSW. 17\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nFor the guidance of Tenderers, a provisional preliminary construction programme is included\nbelow. Selected Subgrade (Upper)\n11. Cemented Subbase\n12. Crushed Stone Base\n13. Surfacing\n14. Ancillary Items\n15. Finishing Road Reserve\n16. Pedestrian Sidewalks\n17. De-Establishment\n18. Road Marking\nEXPECTED CASH FLOWS (in Rands)\nSW. 17\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nC.3.2.24 Management and disposal of water (Read with SANS 1921 - 1: 2004 clause 4.6)\nThe Contractor shall pay special attention to the management and disposal of water and\nstormwater on the site. It is essential that all completed works or parts thereof are kept dry\nand properly drained. Claims for delay and for repair of damage caused to the works as a\nresult of the Contractor’s failure to properly manage rain and surface water, will not be\nconsidered.\nC.3.2.25 Earthworks (Read with SANS 1921 - 1: 2004 clause 4.10)\nBorrow pits and spoil areas\nThe borrow pits to be used for this contract, where possible, shall be pointed out at the\nCompulsory Site Inspection. It is however the contractor’s responsibility to locate the\nclosest borrow pit which will be tested by an approved Geotechnical Engineering specialist\nprior to the source being used. The rate for gravel material shall be deemed to include all\noverhaul based on the identified borrow pits’ distance.\nThe Contractor shall be permitted to use only those borrow pits approved by the Engineer.\nThe spoil sites shall be determined on site in conjunction with the Engineer and the\nMunicipality. The Contractor shall be permitted to use only those spoil areas approved by\nthe Engineer. Should the Contractor wish to use any other tip area for the disposal of soil,\nrubble, vegetation, etc., its use shall be subject to the approval of the Engineer and the\nlandowner.\nC.3.2.26 Testing (Read with SANS 1921 – 1: 2004 clause 4.11)\nProcess control\nThe Contractor shall arrange for all tests required for process control to be done by a\nlaboratory acceptable to and approved by the Engineer.\nThe Contractor may establish his own laboratory on site or he may employ the services of\nan independent commercial laboratory. Whatever method is used, the Contractor must\nsubmit the results of tests carried out on materials and workmanship when submitting work\nfor acceptance by the Engineer. The costs for these tests shall be deemed to be included\nin the relevant rates and no additional payment will be made for testing as required.\nAcceptance control\nThe process control test results submitted by the Contractor for approval of materials and\nworkmanship may be used by the Engineer for acceptance control. However, before\naccepting any work, the Engineer may have further control tests carried out by a laboratory\nof his choice. The cost of such additional tests will be covered by a provisional sum\nprovided in the schedule of quantities, but tests that failed to confirm compliance with the\nspecifications, will be for the account of the Contractor.\nC.3.2.27 Accommodation of Employees\nNo employees except for security guards will be allowed to sleep or be accommodated on\nthe site in urban areas.\nNo housing is available for the Contractor's employees and the Contractor shall make his\nown arrangements to house his employees and to transport them to site.\nNo informal housing or squatting will be allowed.\nSW. 20\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nThe Contractor shall provide the necessary ablution facilities at his camp site and the site\nof the works for the use of his employees. Chemical toilets only will be allowed where\ntemporary facilities have to be provided.\nC.3.2.28 Survey beacons (Read with SANS 1921 - 1: 2004 clause 4.15)\nThe Contractor shall take special precautions to protect all permanent survey beacons or\npegs such as bench-marks, stand boundary pegs and trigonometrical beacons, regardless\nwhether such beacons or pegs were placed before or during the execution of the Contract.\nIf any such beacons or pegs have been disturbed by the Contractor or his employees, the\nContractor shall have them replaced by a registered land surveyor at his own cost.\nC.3.2.29 Health and Safety (Read with SANS 1921 - 1: 2004 clause 4.18)\nC.3.2.29.1 General statement\nIt is a requirement of this contract that the Contractor shall provide a safe and healthy\nworking environment and to direct all his activities in such a manner that his employees\nand any other persons, who may be directly affected by his activities, are not exposed to\nhazards to their health and safety. To this end the Contractor shall assume full\nresponsibility to conform to all the provisions of the Occupational Health and Safety Act No\n85 and Amendment Act No 181 of 1993, and the OHSA 1993 Construction Regulations\n2003 issued on 18 July 2003 by the Department of Labour.\nFor this contract the Contractor is required to confirm his status as mandatory and\nemployer in his own right for the execution of the contract by entering into an agreement\nwith the Employer in terms of the Occupational Health and Safety Act in the form as\nincluded in section C1.2.4\nC.3.2.29.2 Health and Safety Specifications and Plans\n(a) Employer's Health and Safety Specification\nThe Employer's Health and Safety Specification will be included in the tender documents\nas part of the Project Specifications.\n[Note to compiler: The Employer’s Safety Specification is available and must be included]\n(b) Tenderer's Health and Safety Plan\nThe Tenderer shall submit with his tender his own documented Health and Safety Plan he\nproposes to implement for the execution of the work under the contract. His Health and\nSafety Plan must at least cover the following:\ni. a proper risk assessment of the works, risk items, work methods and procedures in\nterms of Regulations 7 to 28;\nii. pro-active identification of potential hazards and unsafe working conditions;\niii. provision of a safe working environment and equipment;\niv. statements of methods to ensure the health and safety of subcontractors, employees\nand visitors to the site, including safety training in hazards and risk areas\n(Regulation 5);\nv. monitoring health and safety on the site of works on a regular basis, and keeping of\nrecords and registers as provided for in the Construction Regulations;\nvi. details of the Construction Supervisor, the Construction Safety Officers and other\ncompetent persons he intends to appoint for the construction works in terms of\nRegulation 6 and other applicable regulations; and vii. details of methods to\nensure that his Health and Safety Plan is carried out effectively in accordance with the\nConstruction Regulations 2003.\nSW. 21\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nThe Contractor's Health and Safety Plan will be subject to approval by the Employer, or\namendment, if necessary, before commencement of construction work. The Contractor will\nnot be allowed to commence work, or his work will be suspended if he had already\ncommenced work, before he has obtained the Employer's written approval of his Health\nand Safety Plan.\nTime lost due to delayed commencement or suspension of the work as a result of the\nContractor's failure to obtain approval for his safety plan, shall not be used as a reason to\nclaim for extension of time or standing time and related costs\nC.3.2.29.3 Cost of compliance with the OHSA Construction Regulations\nThe rates and prices tendered by the Contractor shall be deemed to include all costs for\nconforming to the requirements of the Act, the Construction Regulations and the\nEmployer's Health and Safety Specification as applicable to this contract.\nShould the Contractor fail to comply with the provisions of the Construction Regulations,\nhe\nwill be liable for penalties as provided in the Construction Regulations and in the\nEmployer's Health and Safety Specification.\nC.3.2.29.4 Management of the environment (Read with SANS 1921 - 1: 2004 clause 4.19)\nRespect for the environment is an important aspect of this contract and the Contractor\nshall pay special attention to the following:\n(a) Natural Vegetation\nThe Contractor shall confine his operation to the limits of the road reserve for constructing\nthe works and where applicable detours, shall be sited in consultation with the Engineer\nand the local communities.\nOnly those trees and shrubs directly affected by the works and those the Engineer may\ndirectly instruct, shall be cut down and stumped. The natural vegetation, grassing and\nother plants shall not be disturbed other than in areas where it is essential for the\nexecution of the work or where directed by the Engineer.\n(b) Fires\nThe Contractor shall comply with the statutory and local fire regulations. He shall also take\nall necessary precautions to prevent any fires. In the event of fire, the Contractor shall take\nactive steps to limit and extinguish the fire and shall accept full responsibility for damages\nand claims resulting from such fires which may have been caused by him or his\nemployees.\n(c) Environmental Management Plan\nIn addition to the above all requirements according to the Environmental Management\nFramework prepared by the Municipality, and the project specific Environmental\nManagement Plan will be adhered to.\nC.3.2.30 EPWP Alignment Clauses\nC.3.2.30.1 (1.2) Personal & Other Protective Equipment (Sections 8/15/23 or the OHS Act)\nThe Contractor is required to identify the hazards in the workplace and deal with them. He\nmust either remove them or, where impracticable take steps to protect workers and make it\npossible for them to work safely and without risk to health under the hazardous conditions.\nSW. 22\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nPersonal Protective equipment (PPE) should, however, be the last resort and there should\nalways first be an attempt to apply engineering and other solutions to mitigating hazardous\nsituations before the issuing of PPE is considered.\nWhere it is not possible to create an absolutely safe and healthy workplace the Contractor\nis required to inform employees regarding this and issue, free of charge, suitable\nequipment to protect them from any hazards being present and that allows them to work\nsafely and without risk to health in the hazardous environment.\nIt is a further requirement that the said equipment be maintained by the Contractor, that he\ninstructs and trains the employees in the use of the equipment and ensures that the\nprescribed equipment is used by the employee/s.\nEmployees do not have the right to refuse to use/wear the equipment prescribed by the\nemployer and, if it is impossible for an employee to use or wear prescribed protective\nequipment through health or any other reason, the employee cannot be allowed to\ncontinue working under the hazardous condition/s for which the equipment was prescribed\nbut an alternative solution has to be found that may include relocating or discharging the\nemployee.\nThe Contractor may not charge any fee for protective equipment prescribed by him/her but\nmay charge for equipment under the following conditions:\n• Where the employee requests additional issue in excess of what is prescribed\n• Where the employee has patently abused or neglected the equipment leading to early\nfailure\n• Where the employee has lost the equipment\nAll employees shall, as a minimum, be required to wear the following PPE on any\ninfrastructure projects:\nProtective overalls\n• Protective footwear\n• Protective headwear\n• Eye/face protection\nAll PPE provided to local labour working on the Expanded Public Works Programme shall\nbe branded in accordance with the EPWP CI Manual. Typical elements which shall be\nbranded include: • Protective overalls\n• Reflective vests\n• Protective headwear\nThe rate for local labour shall include for the supply of EPWP branded PPE in accordance\nwith the Provincial EPWP specifications. The rate shall include the additional cost of the\nspecified colours for the PPE and branding in accordance with CI manual.\nC.3.2.30.2 (B1231) COMMUNITY LIAISON OFFICER (CLO)\nThe contractor or his appointed agent will appoint a Community Liaison Officer (CLO) after\nconsultation with the local communities, the engineer and the employer. The contractor\nshall direct all his liaison efforts with the local communities through the appointed officer.\nThe contractor shall, however, accept the appointed as part of his management personnel.\n(a) Duties of the Community Liaison Officer\nThe Community Liaison Officer’s duties will be:\n(i) To be available on site daily between the hours of (insert time) and (insert time)\nand at other time as the need arises. His normal working day will extend from\nmorning until (insert time) in the afternoon.\n(ii) To determine, in consultation with the contractor, the needs of the temporary\nlabour for relevant skills training. He will be responsible for the identification of\nsuitable trainees and will attend one of each of the training sessions.\n(iii) To communicate daily with the contractor and the engineer to determine the\nlabour requirements regarding numbers and skill, to facilitate in labour disputes\nand to assist in their resolution.\nSW. 23\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\n(iv) To assist in and facilitate in the recruitment of suitable temporary labour and the\nestablishment of a “labour desk”.\n(v) To attend all meetings in which the community and/or labour are present or are\nrequired to be represented.\n(vi) To assist in the identification, and screening of labourers from the community in\naccordance with the contractor’s requirements.\n(vii) To inform temporary labour of their conditions of temporary employment and to\ninform temporary labourers as early as possible when their period of employment\nwill be terminated.\n(viii) To attend disciplinary proceedings to ensure that hearings are fair and\nreasonable.\n(ix) To keep a daily written record of his interviews and community liaison.\n(x) To attend monthly site meetings to report on labour and RDP matters.\n(xi) All such other duties as agreed upon between all parties concerned. (xii) To\nsubmit monthly returns regarding community liaison.\n(b) Payment for the community liaison officer\nA special pay item is incorporated in section 1200 of the bill of quantities relating to\npayment of the liaison officer on a prime cost sum basis. This payment shall only be made\nfor the period for which the duties of the liaison officer are required and not necessarily for\nthe full duration of the contract. The remuneration of the CLO shall be determined jointly by\nthe contractor, engineer and employer.\n(c) Period of employment of the community liaison officer\nThe period of employment of the community liaison officer shall be as decided upon jointly\nby the contractor, engineer and employer.\nSW. 24\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27 PART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION - WARD 3\nPART B: AMENDMENTS TO THE STANDARD SPECIFICATIONS\n• SECTION B1: STANDARD AMENDMENTS ISSUED BY COTO\nNotes to tenderer:\n1. The Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Works for South African Road Authorities\n(Draft Standard October 2020 edition) prepared by the Committee of Transport Officials,\n(COTO), as amended, shall apply to this contract. The amendments are those issued by COTO\nand reproduced in Section B1, together with additional amendments as set out in Section B2\nand Project specific Specification Data as set out in Section B3.\nAs at February 2025 no amendments have been issued.by COTO.\nSW. 25\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nSECTION B2: PROJECT SPECIFICATION AMENDMENTS TO THE COTO STANDARD\nSPECIFICATIONS\nNotes to tenderer:\n1. Details of such alternatives or additional requirements applicable to this contract are contained in\nSection B2: Specification Data. Section B2 also contains project specific sections for Sections C, D\nand E.\n1. The number of each clause and each payment item in this part of the project specifications follows the\nnumbering format of the standard specifications.\nSW. 26\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nTABLE OF CONTENTS PAGE\nCOTO CHAPTER 1: GENERAL C3-26\nCOTO CHAPTER 2: SERVICES C3-32\nCOTO CHAPTER 3: DRAINAGE C3-33\nCOTO CHAPTER 4: EARTHWORKS AND PAVEMENT LAYERS: MATERIALS C3-34\nCOTO CHAPTER 5: EARTHWORKS AND PAVEMENT LAYERS: CONSTRUCTION C3-35\nCOTO CHAPTER 6: CONCRETE LAYERS C3-36\nCOTO CHAPTER 7: MAINTENANCE AND REPAIR OF CONCRETE LAYERS C3-37\nCOTO CHAPTER 8: PRETREATMENT AND REPAIR OF EXISTING LAYERS C3-38\nCOTO CHAPTER 9: ASPHALT LAYERS C3-39\nCOTO CHAPTER 10: SURFACE TREATMENTS C3-41\nCOTO CHAPTER 11: ANCILLARY ROAD WORKS C3-43\nCOTO CHAPTER 12: GEOTECHNICAL APPLICATIONS C3-44\nCOTO CHAPTER 13: STRUCTURES C3-46\nCOTO CHAPTER 14: REPAIR AND REHABILITATION OF STRUCTURESC3-ERROR! BOOKMARK NOT DEFINED.\nCOTO CHAPTER 20: QUALITY ASSURANCE C3-48\nSECTION B2: PROJECT SPECIFICATION\n• COTO CHAPTER 1: GENERAL\nSECTION 1.1: GENERAL PREAMBLE\nPART A: SPECIFICATIONS\nA1.1.2 DEFINITIONS\nReplace the Definition for “Site / Site of the Works” with the following:\n“Site / Site of the Works - shall mean the entire road reserve (both new and existing), inclusive\nof road junctions and property accesses, required for construction of the Works as defined by\nthe limits of construction given in the Contract Documentation. It shall also include areas within\nstatutory building lines where work has to be carried out and any additional lengths of road\nrequired for the placement of advanced warning road signs and/or traffic accommodation\nmeasures beyond the limits of construction as shown on the drawings. The Site shall also\ninclude areas outside of the road reserve required for Construction camps, Engineer’s site\nfacilities, Borrow pit areas or quarry areas, haulage and access roads, temporary deviations,\nstorage areas, spoil areas and stockpile areas. The exact extent of the limits of the construction\nwill be verified once the Site is handed over to the Contractor.”\nPART C: MEASUREMENT AND PAYMENT\nSW. 27\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nC1.1.3 PAYMENT\nC1.1.3.5 Payment for materials on the Site\nIn the last sentence of the 1st paragraph, delete the following:\n“, or, in the case of crushed stone which has not been purchased but has been produced on the\nsite, at 80% of a fair evaluation of such crushed material”.\nAdd the following new subclauses:\n"C1.1.3.9 Reduced payments for substandard work\nWhere provision for reduced payments for sub-standard work is made in the Contract\nDocumentation, acceptance of reduce payment for substandard work may be accepted by the\nEngineer subject to prior approval by the Employer.\nC1.1.3.10 Procurement of sub-services and omitted rates (Second tier procurement)\nSecond tier procurement include the procurement of any work where either the particulars of\nthe work is not scheduled and priced, or where the process of procurement of the sub-service\nprovider is specified elsewhere in the contract specification. It includes the procurement of work\nwhere rates have been omitted or where allowance for the work is made under a Provisional\nsum or Prime sum item or where allowance for the work is made under a Provisional sum or\nPrime sum item but the particulars of the work is not scheduled, or where work is instructed\nunder clause 6.3 [Variations] or where work is to be performed by Targeted Enterprises.\nThe following procurement methods is to be followed as appropriate:\na) Where the particulars of the work are not scheduled but existing rates for similar\nwork exist in the contract and the work can therefore be executed by the contractor\nor his sub-contractor at the existing contract rates.\nNo separate procurement process is required. The work is to be quantified and\nscheduled utilising existing rates and approved through the Works Authorisation process.\nb) Where the payment calculation is based on a formula specified in the contract\ndocument, or\nwhere the payment rate is pre-determined or fixed by the client.\nNo separate procurement process is required. The work is to be quantified and approved\nthrough the Works Authorisation process.\nc) Where the supplier is not selected by the contractor and actual cost is\nreimbursable and no procurement process is possible.\nNo separate procurement process is required. The work is invoiced by supplier on\ncompletion and approved through the Works Authorisation process at the end of the\ncontract.\nd) Where there are omitted items as part of the existing scheduled scope of work and\nno existing rates for similar work exist in the contract, or\nwhere there are no existing rates for the materials to be supplied and suitable rates\nfor material to be determined.\nSW. 28\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nA proposal for a new rate shall be submitted by the contractor and evaluated by the\nengineer, by comparing with either adjusted relevant rates in the contract, or by\ncomparing with similar rates on similar contracts, or by comparing three informal\nquotes\nto substantiate the rate. The new agreed rate is approved through the Works\nAuthorisation process.\ne) Where the particulars of the work are not scheduled and the estimated cost of the\nwork (including VAT and excluding Contract Price Adjustment) is equal or less than\nR500,000.00 and there are no existing rates for similar work and the contractor’s\nproposal submitted in terms of GCC Variation 6.3 is not accepted and the work is\nto be performed by a sub-contractor.\nA minimum of three quotations shall be obtained from Targeted Enterprises (as defined in\nSection D1000). 3\nPART A: SPECIFICATIONS\nA1.2.3 GENERAL\nA1.2.3.4 Extension of time for delays caused by rainfall\nExtension of time resulting from abnormal rainfall shall be calculated according to the\nrequirements of Method (ii) (Critical-path method).\nAdd the following to the end of Method (ii):\n"The value of “n” working days expected delay caused by normal rainy weather as referred to in\nMethod (ii) shall be as given in Table A1.2.3.4 for each respective calendar month of any year:\nTable A1.2.3.4\nMonth Expected delay of "n" Month Expected delay of "n"\nworking days due to normal working days due to normal\nrainy weather rainy weather\nJanuary*** 4 July 1\nFebruary 4 August 1\nMarch 4 September 2\nApril 3 October 3\nMay 2 November 4\nJune 1 December*** 4\n*** Includes the whole month of December / January.\nEach "n"-value in Table A1.2.3.4 applies only to the calendar month immediately to the left of\nthe number, and the "n"-values as specified shall not be taken as being carried forward so as to\naccumulate over the contract period. If no abnormal rainfall occurs during a particular calendar\nmonth in a particular year, then no extension of time for abnormal rainfall shall be granted with\nrespect to that calendar month for that year, and no further consideration shall be given to that\n"n"-value in respect of that year.\nSimilarly, if the "n" working days expected delay caused by normal rainy weather during a\nparticular calendar month in a particular year (for which the Contractor shall have made\nprovision in his programme of work in accordance with Method (ii)) are not taken up (either in\nwhole or in part) by standing time due to normal rainy weather during that month of that year,\nthen no further consideration shall be given to those "n" working days (or portion thereof), which\neffectively have been gained, when any subsequent extension of time claims which may arise\nlater during the contract period are assessed by the Employer’s Agent."\nA1.2.3.15 Routine maintenance\nAdd the following new paragraphs:\n“The backfilling for patching shall be done as indicated in the Contract Documentation.”\nThe riding quality of gravel deviations shall comply with the following the requirements indicated\nin the Contract Documentation.”\nSW. 30\n\nCONTRACT NO.: GKM 05-26/27\nPART C3: SCOPE OF WORK\nTHE AVENUE ROAD REHABILITATION WARD - 3\nAdd the following new subclause:\n"A1.2.3.24 Reference Manuals, other specifications and test methods\nIn various chapters of this Standard Specification, reference is made to Manuals, other\nspecifications, and test methods.
